# Cinders of Chaos roadmap

Priority order agreed with the project owner. Update the status here when something is started or done.
Status: `todo`, `in progress`, `done`.

## Next: fix the late game (players hit a wall after level ~500)

Estimated before items 4 and 5: ~155 h to level 1000 for a sorcerer, ~285 h for a knight or paladin, and 80% of it is spent between 700 and 1000.

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | Spawn the level-scaled Chaos monsters on the map that is actually loaded | done | 18 of them (levels 650-1000, retuned health/speed/damage/exp, weak to physical and holy) replace 277 of the weaker spawns in four level 500+ areas (Nightglass Core, Jaded Roots, Gloom Pillars, Putrefactory): `server/tools/late_game.py` + `late_game_spawns.csv`, README "Late game". Dawn Strayer (art 2122) joined tier 3 in Nightglass Core with 9 spawns, retuned from its donor file |
| 5 | Smooth the experience stage curve after level 700 | done | x15 to 800, x14 to 900, x13 after (was 12/9/6/3; the last stage had no maxlevel, which this engine reads as 0, so 851+ really got rateExp x5). Model: sorcerer ~96 h, knight ~154 h, paladin ~140 h to 1000 (was ~138/257/252 h with the x5 bug, ~154/286/281 h as written) |
| 6 | Fix the waystone "Experience" character upgrade (and its siblings Loot and Rarity) | done | all three only wrote a storage nothing read; now applied (`waystone_bonuses.lua`, `tools/patch_base_events.py`) |
| 7 | Monster level bonus roll bug | done (kept as is) | `src/monster.cpp:52` uses `\|\|`, so only the first branch runs: a plain uniform roll between min and max level. The other branches would skew 80% of monsters to the top of their range and compute negative ranges for narrow level spans, so the current uniform behaviour is kept on purpose |
| 89 | Monster density, respawn and map capacity for the launch population | done (built and run on the full map with a test database; real players not yet) | measured on the loaded map: 5.36 million walkable tiles, 74,071 spawn entries, 1,195 hunting areas; capacity per level band from the leveling model (server/README.md "Spawn density and respawn"). The 300-600 band was the shortage of launch week 1 (about 96% load at 400 online): 913 entries of 10 monsters never loaded (wrong-case `monsters.xml` lines), 111 entries respawned once in 9 hours, 99 sat on blocking tiles. `tools/patch_base_spawns.py` + `spawn_tuning.json` (install step `spawns`) fix those, set the 300-600 band to 90 s and add 184 spawns in 21 thin areas; `prod_config.py` sets `rateSpawn = 3`; dynamic respawn (`respawn*.lua`, engine patch 0022): 85% down to 50% of the spawntime where 2-5+ players hunt together. 300-600 capacity 141 -> 249 hunters (55% load in week 1). Open: walk check of the closed pockets and over-dense strips; 1000+ has 6 areas (the 900+ areas and the instanced endgame carry it); new level 1000-1300 areas only if 30+ such players are online after month 1. The gap is covered by three generated 1000-1300 areas ready to switch on (row 109: 205 monsters + 3 bosses, about 40 more hunters). Fill (2026-10-14, owner request "every hunt big and full of monsters"): `tools/gen_spawn_density.py` measured every base-map hunt on the map and `spawn_density.json` adds 10,922 one-monster spawns in 570 thin hunts (to 3.4 per 100 walkable tiles, at most x2 the entries), so all but 6 hunts outside closed pockets are at 3.25+ (those 6 hit the x2 or +400 cap; 4 stay under 3.0); capacity of the model +15-58% per band (server/README.md "Spawn density and respawn", Fill). Physically small hunts (under 20 monsters) need a map extension: list in that section. Respawn near players (2026-10-16, owner request): engine patch 0041, config `spawnNearPlayers` (on) and `spawnMinPlayerDistance` (2): a block respawns while a player sees it, on a free non-PZ tile not next to a player, with a teleport effect (built against the chain; not run live; server/README.md "Respawn near players") |
| 109 | Procedural area generator: caves, room-and-corridor dungeons and open wastes from a seed, built at startup like the rifts; three endgame hunting areas for levels 1000-1300 and one generated dungeon of the dungeon system (owner approval) | done (unit and mocked-engine tests; built on a test server with the cropped map and walked with the Linux client; the entrances on the full map not run) | `chaos/areagen_core.lua` (pure: layouts, floors joined by hole and ladder, borders per neighbour pattern, walls, decoration, lights, furniture, spawns, boss room, validator) + `areagen_config.lua` (palettes measured on the live map with `tools/map/derive_borders.py`, areas) + `areagen.lua` (build, portals with action ids 47950/47951 and a level gate, scaled spawns with their own respawn and the dynamic respawn percent, leash, dungeon registration; one hook in `dungeons.lua`); `tools/areagen_export.py` (OTBM piece and spawn file for the map editor, item check against the live items.otb and pair, previews), `tests/areagen_test.lua`, `tools/areagen_export_test.py`. Areas: Emberdeep Hollows (cave, 2 floors, 1000-1100, 65 monsters), Sunken Ossuary (ruins, 2 floors, 1100-1200, 68), Cinderfall Wastes (waste, 1200-1300, 72), The Hollow Vault (dungeon 5, level 800). Previews in `docs/map_guide/generated/`; server/README.md "Generated areas", docs/MAP_GUIDE.md "Generated areas". Rift maps (2026-10-12): four rift themes, the `mixed` kind, coarse-grid caves for big layouts, per-kind theme overrides, arena and link options, a tick hook so a coroutine can build a 240 x 240 map in short steps; the four areas unchanged (same checksums); `areagen_export.py` previews and checks rift maps (`--area rift:...`, 280 ids, 0 problems). Open: entrances checked on the map data only; balance of the scaled monsters not measured; monster names show their native level. Grown (2026-10-14, owner request "big and full"): each area x2.0-2.2 walkable tiles and 3.25 monsters per 100: Emberdeep 7,348 tiles / 220 monsters, Ossuary 5,678 / 172, Cinderfall 11,156 / 346, the Vault 2,169 / 64 (run 40 minutes, was 30) |

To check live: server/README.md "Player summons are allies". Second pass 2026-10-27: a likely cause, not verified (no engine source here): the engine lists the owner, the party and the owner's other summons in every summon's target list (placed before `setMaster`; `isOpponent` of a summon is "everybody but the master"; `updateTargetList` never drops non-opponents) and the scripts only refused the hit. Now `ChaosSummonsCore.purge` / `purgeAll` remove allies from the engine target list (`getTargetList` / `removeTarget`, all behind `pcall`) at `bind` and every 0.5 s (covers relog, teleport, town portal, party join, PvP zone changes), the follow target is never touched, and `sameParty` compares leaders when `getParty()` hands out a new object per call; mocked tests for each path in `tests/summons_test.lua`. The engine source confirms it: `Monster::isOpponent` of a player's summon was "everybody but the master". Engine patch `0086-summon-allies-not-opponents.patch`: the owner, its other summons, its party and their summons are no opponents; `updateTargetList` drops a creature that is no opponent any more. A spin: 1.16 orbs + 717k gold at level 1000 (788 + 66 kills of orb and gold) against 0 before; cards 6.4% -> 1.0%. **Uniques**: not bugged in the code, three causes (name lists without hunt monsters, the thin pack factor, the level gate reading 0); fixed with `unique.open`, `uniquePace`, `monsterLevel`: Rare / Legendary kills at level 1500 give 17.4 uniques per 1000 (was 0.65), one every ~3.4 hours; pity (x3) already applies. Open: the roulette value against the token drop rate; the other 4 roulettes untouched. |

| O146 | Beta feedback 2026-10-23 (level 1500): stats over their caps are wasted, Paragon fills by level 1100-1200 and ~300 points are left, gear crit is not shown in the tree window | done (unit tested, not measured in game; server: `files` step, a restart; client update; no DB migration) | Confirmed: attribute and damage-percent caps were hard (`passivetree_effects.lua`), Paragon had no sink, the window showed node sums only. Soft caps instead (server/README.md "Soft caps, Paragon Mastery and totals (O146)"): full value to the cap, then `cap + H ln(1 + (raw - cap) / H)`; absorb (75) and speed stay hard. Paragon Mastery: seven endless ranks (1 point each, `scale ln(1 + n/h)`) plus an exchange of free tree points for Paragon points. New `statTotals` message and a totals section in the tree window; `tools/crit_reach.lua` computes the reachable crit chance (dedicated build about 68, threshold for a gated passive 45). Monsters of a run scale with its size: health x (1 + 0.6 (n-1)), damage to the players x (1 + 0.1 (n-1)); the numbers of every dungeon are the measured solo ones, so 1 player = x1.0 (`dungeons_difficulty.lua` `D.partyHealth` / `D.partyDamage`, `tests/dungeons_difficulty_test.lua`, `tests/dungeons_area_test.lua`). Respawn bug: the map respawn was already refused during a run (owner 2026-10-17); one gap found and fixed: a won run kept its exit delay (10 s) with `ended` set, in which the map could respawn the boss and the trash next to the group (`runAt` now holds until teardown); the boss is created once (unlock once) and nothing is created after the run opens, tested. Beta test mode: `Chaos.betaMode` (`beta_mode_config.lua` `enabled`, default off) lifts 8 weekly / daily gameplay limits (list in server/README.md "Beta test mode"); never the store's `weeklyLimit` offers (real money) unless the owner names an offer in `storeOfferKeys` (empty). `chaos-ctl.sh beta-mode on|off|status [restart]`, admin app Comandos > Modo beta (testes) > Ligar / Desligar; `update-apply` keeps the switch (`install_all.py` `keep_switch`). Open: the Orb of Binding sold in the store (3 a week, Chaos Coins) stays capped; to lift it for the beta put `"item:32252x1"` in `storeOfferKeys` (owner decision) |

Common lines per class were 2 to 7 (median 3): Illusionist, Thief line, Monk line, Templar line had 2 and the Necromancer line 3; now every class has at least 3 and the Necromancer line 4 (`also` spells: Marrow Bolt and Soulrend Beam on Rime Bloom, Radiant Smite on Dawnpiercer, Shattered Psyche on Frozen Crypt, Shuriken Toss on Primordial Squall, Palm Shockwave on Earthbreaker); `cards_test.lua` keeps the floor. | O153 | Engine hardening part (3), owner 2026-10-20: new services outside the engine may be written in Rust, one at a time | done for the first service (unit and property tests under cargo, the same 50 requests against both servers, the switch tested with a stub build; not built or run on the VPS; server: `chaos-ctl.sh clientservices-impl rust`; admin app rebuild for the new card; no engine or client change) | **Which**: of the three named, the login gateway is HAProxy and the payment worker is PHP (outgoing calls only), the Help assistant is Python but never accepts a connection. The only Python program with a public listener is the login-screen services endpoint (`tools/clientservices.py`, `0.0.0.0:8090`), so that one was ported: `server/services/clientservices-rs/` (`forbid(unsafe_code)`, `serde_json` only, `Cargo.lock` committed, credits added). Same behaviour and command line; stricter limits (30 s per request, 256 connections, 128 KiB of headers). **Opt-in**: `chaos-ctl.sh clientservices-impl [status | rust | python]` builds on the VPS (cargo needed), adds a systemd drop-in and restarts only that unit, falls back to python if it does not stay up; admin app Comandos > Serviços da tela de login (Rust); `chaos_update.sh` rebuilds while on. CI: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --locked`, `compare_test.py`. server/README.md "Rust services (O153)". cargo-fuzz run 2026-10-27 (nightly, libFuzzer, `fuzz/`: `read_head` 10 min, 2,294,629 execs; `parse_misc` 10 min, 1,816,311 execs; seeds replayed on stable in `cargo test`): no crash, panic, hang or broken invariant, no code change. Open: a build on the VPS, the payment worker and the Help assistant (neither has a network surface; port only if asked), the Linux kit keeps Python |
| O154 | Owner 2026-10-24 ("pode acelerar 5x o treino de skill"), the decision O150 left open: a character that hunts to level 1000 trains its main skill to about 105 (the C4 measurements assume 140) | done (computed with `skill_model.lua`, not measured in game; server: `install_all.py SERVER_DIR --only skillstages` (the admin app's Update runs it) and a restart; no client change, no SQL) | `server/tools/patch_skill_stages.py` multiplies every skill and magic level stage multiplier of the base `scripts/customs/skills/skills_stages.lua` from level 101 up by 5 (`skill_stages_config.lua`: `fromLevel 101`, `factor 5`; stages up to 100 stay; skills x45 / x40 / x35 / x20, magic x35 / x30 / x25 / x15). Idempotent (`-- Chaos: was N` on each changed row, a new factor derives from those), CRLF kept, an unknown shape refused; test `patch_skill_stages_test.py`. `skill_model.lua` reads the same config: Warrior 100 to 130 by hunting 1,495 to 304 hours (Ranger 182, Mystic 269, Thief and Monk 228), main skill after the 139 hours of leveling 107-112 to 117-124, 100 to 130 on the dummy 84 to 17 hours. A dummy hour is worth the same hunting hours, so `store_model.lua --check` and the prices are unchanged; the coins to train 100 to 130 fall from about 12,600 to 2,700 (a smaller coin sink, watch it). `weapon_skill_config.lua` `typical` (140 at level 1000) not retuned: hunting alone is still 117-124, equipment and the tree close the rest. docs/PRICES.md "Skill stages x5 (O154)" |
| O156 | Owner 2026-10-24 (live screenshot): the helper's Support tab Auto setup still filled Greater Lantern, Ghost Rope and Lantern after the name fix of 2026-10-23 | done (client unit tests under luajit and lua5.3, the regression fails on the old code; not run in a live client) | **Root cause**: the client's spell table is a map name -> entry and most entries carry no `name` field (the key is the name), so the classifier saw an empty name and, with the stock words, let them through; Auto setup then planned them as cheap unknown support spells, and 'add' mode and the saved list kept them. **Fix**: one classifier (`ChaosHelperSupport.isUtility`, names compared lower case with single spaces) used by Auto setup, the dropdown, the cast, the helper's pickers and the load; `H.entries` gives every entry its name from the key; Auto setup never plans a utility or a spell it cannot name; `setSlots` drops utilities whoever asks; the saved list is cleaned at load as before. Files: `client/overlay/modules/game_helpersupport/` (`supportspells.lua`, `supportspells_ui.lua`, test), `game_helper/helper.lua`, `helper_spells.lua`. Needs a client update |
| O157 | Owner 2026-10-24 (live client): the spell list showed no icon for Gutting Slash, Razor Rain, Venom Spiral, Crescent Cut and Last Breath, and the helper's Auto setup did not add them to a Thief's rotation | done (client unit tests under luajit and lua5.3, the new checks fail on the broken cases; not run in a live client) | **Icons**: the repository was already right (all 148 spells of `spelldata.lua` have a drawn cell in the three sheets since O133, which regenerated them, `gen_all_spell_icons.py` reproduces them byte for byte, and the client's own lookup resolves every spell in a mocked run), so the live client most likely ran a build older than the server's O133 spells (the spell list is the server's, the icon table is the client's). **New check** `tools/ci/check_spell_icons.py` (stdlib PNG reader, with a test): every spell of the server XML is in `spelldata.lua`, no shared cell or key, and in each of the three sheets the cell exists, is not transparent and is not the filler plate. **Auto setup**: the rotation data only knew spells a preset used and the top-up only drew from presets, so Gutting Slash (never best against Shuriken Toss), Cleaving Heel, Tidal Palm, Zealot's Wrath, Rampart Quake and 12 more were never in a rotation. `rotation_sim.py`: free slots take a spell that keeps 97% of the score (`fill_slots`), `spells` lists every attack spell, `extras` per class holds the ones no preset has, and the client's top-up uses them last. **New check** `tools/ci/check_rotation_kit.py` (with a test): every kit spell is known with the area rule of its shape and every attack spell of every class is in a preset or the extras; Thief regression in `autorotation_test.lua`. Files: `tools/ci/check_spell_icons*.py`, `check_rotation_kit*.py`, `server/tools/rotation_sim.py`, `game_helperrotation/` (`autorotation.lua`, `autorotation_data.lua`, test), `ci.yml`, `CLAUDE.md`. Needs a client update |
| O158 | Owner request: Blackblood Creed (Thief line keystone, node 383) poisons on EVERY attack, not only weapon hits: auto attacks and all attack spells, single target and area | done (unit tests under luajit and lua5.3; not run in a live client) | Same `onHealthChange` hook as Poison Blades (`thief_combat.lua` `onOffence`), now also for `ORIGIN_SPELL` and `ORIGIN_WAND` (`ChaosClasses.creedPoisons`). Never for `ORIGIN_CONDITION` (the poison's own ticks and other DoTs), `ORIGIN_NONE` (scripted, reflected), healing, zero or self damage. One poison per target per hit, the same rule as Poison Blades. Poison +30% and evasion halved unchanged. Node text in `nodes.lua`, wiki regenerated. Needs a server restart and, for the new node text, a client update. Balance pass done 2026-10-26 (computed, not measured in game; `server/data/spells/README.md` "Blackblood Creed on every attack"): the only new poison sources are Venom Spiral ticks and Smoke Bomb (the line's other spells are weapon strikes and poisoned already); totals move -1.2% to +0.8% on one target and -2.0% to +2.4% on five, the Ninja stays 6.2-6.9% behind the Deadeye at level 1000, no class passes a leader, no number changed (`poisonBonus` 0.30). Open: the Creed on five targets was never measured, the model has the level 1000 Ninja only 4-11% behind the Elementalist in area; measure it (Ninja, two samples) before touching `poisonBonus`. |
| O160 | Owner 2026-10-24 (live screenshot): the NPC dialogue window (Loot Buyer) showed "You: hi" and then only "...", the NPCs' lines never appeared | done (client unit tests under luajit and lua5.3, the routing tests fail on the old code; a mocked-module run of the whole path; not run in a live client) | **Cause**: the bubble got its lines only from one place, the hook the base console calls inside `Chat:onTalk` (`game_npctrade.tryHandleNpcDialogMessage`), after the console's own map text and checks; anything that stopped that call short, or a speaker name that did not equal the clicked NPC's name to the letter, left the bubble waiting at "..." and the line nowhere. The routing was also inline in `npctalk.lua`, untested. **Fix**: `NpcTalkLogic.routeNpcLine` decides where a line goes (NPC modes only; the open conversation's NPC is known by name ignoring case, spacing and edge punctuation, or by its tile); the module also listens to the game's own `onTalk` signal, so the bubble no longer depends on the console, and `isDuplicate` makes the two copies of a line one (the hook still keeps the line out of the console). Keywords stay clickable. Files: `client/overlay/modules/game_npctalk/` (`npctalk.lua`, `npctalk_logic.lua`, `npctalk_logic_test.lua`). Needs a client update, no server restart |
| O159 | Owner 2026-10-24 (live client, Thief Udido): the death recap lists "Yourself, 5 hits, mana, Mana drain 1.74k" | done (server unit tests under luajit and lua5.3, the new checks fail on the old code; not run live; server restart, no client change) | **Root cause**: `potions.lua` drinks a mana potion as `doTargetCombat(player, who, COMBAT_MANADRAIN, +v, +v)`, a restore with the drinker as attacker. The `ChaosCombatLogMana` event (`combatlog.lua`, mana drain branch) fed every `COMBAT_MANADRAIN` change to the recap with `abs()`, so each potion became a mana hit by the character on itself (5 potions = 5 hits, 1,748 mana). **Fix**: the branch counts only a negative (draining) value; `deathrecap.lua` `R.onTaken` drops a self-inflicted manadrain or healing hit as a second guard, so "Yourself" never lists a restore or cost. A real drain by a monster still shows. Tests: `combatlog_test.lua`, `deathrecap_test.lua`. server/README.md "Death recap" |
| O162 | Owner report (live, Elementalist level ~230): "You lose 1.55k hitpoints due to an attack by orcaruz" with the mana shield up, one hit | done (unit test under luajit and lua5.3; not run live) | Two causes. (1) Soulward added its mana shield with sub id 71006 and the engine only sees sub id 0: no shield at all. `soul_aegis.lua` adds the sub id 0 shield plus the marker; buff bar hides the duplicate row. (2) Orcaruz hit up to 2,500 at level 200 (about 1,295 health for a level 230 Mystic): strongest hit now 650 (`tune_monsters.py`). Spells reload, no engine, no client change. Test `soulward_shield_test.lua`. |

## Open decisions and loose ends

- The 12-month moonshot plan (owner 2026-10-27, not scheduled): living world, play the boss, phone client. See
  `docs/MOONSHOT.md`.
- Parts (1) and (2) done 2026-10-23, part (3) started (O153) (owner 2026-10-20, later: "Coloque as suas recomendações ... de
  rust"): engine hardening instead of a Rust rewrite (a rewrite would mean the whole base engine, the src-patches and
  the Lua API at the same behaviour, for little a player sees). (1) Done: `server/tools/build_sanitized.sh`, a debug
  build with AddressSanitizer / UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer, run on the full map under 100 and 50 load test bots.
  (2) Done: libFuzzer harnesses of the PROXY protocol parser (0025), the client guard (0021), the failed-login limit
  (0047) and the login / game packet reads (`server/tools/fuzz/`, seed corpus run in CI), and the network fuzzer
  `server/tools/netfuzz.py` against the sanitizer build. Found and fixed: src-patches 0061 (shutdown use-after-free in
  the scheduler), 0062 (Lua environment built before use), 0063 (the first packet read up to 8 bytes past its end),
  install step `jsonguards` (base opcode handlers and non-object JSON). server/README.md "Engine hardening
  (sanitizers, fuzzing)". (3) First service done 2026-10-23 (O153): the login-screen services endpoint
  (`clientservices-rs`, opt-in switch). The login gateway is HAProxy and the payment worker is PHP, so they are not Python
  ports. Open: the other services one at a time if wanted (Help assistant); a sanitizer run on the VPS with real players.
- TODO (owner 2026-10-24, later; "Coloque o client em Godot no TODO"): a new game client in Godot 4 next to the
  current one, the server unchanged (C++, protocol 10.98 plus the JSON extended opcodes). Plan when started: (1) a
  prototype that logs in (RSA, XTEA, checksum), walks and draws the map, floors and creatures from our .dat/.spr or a
  converted atlas; (2) run it beside the current client on the same server; (3) port the modules one by one only if
  the prototype is kept. Gains: shaders, light and particles, Android and web builds, simpler UI than .otui. Costs:
  the login security and the encrypted client pack redone, the base patches and every client module rewritten, two
  clients kept in step for a while. Not started.
- TODO (owner 2026-10-20, later; "Eu prefiro a 3. Coloque no TODO por enquanto"): a live spectator view of a player
  in the admin app (android/): the real game view with sprites (the .dat/.spr on the device) fed by a server cast /
  spectator system (engine patch). The simpler options (a JSON map of the surroundings, a client screenshot with a
  notice to the player) were offered and not chosen.
  In progress, phase 1 (2026-10-23, unit tested only): a Lua snapshot of the 15 x 11 view (no engine patch) drawn
  into a PNG on the VPS with the real pair (`server/tools/spectate_render.py`, `chaos-ctl.sh spectate NAME`, the pair
  fetched by `spectate-pair fetch`) and shown in the app's Comandos > Ações rápidas > Assistir jogador about once a
  second (server/README.md "Live spectator", docs/ADMIN_APP.md). It differs from the chosen option: the sprites are
  drawn on the VPS, not on the device, and there is no engine cast; no animation, light or effects. Not run on a real
  server, with the real pair or on a phone.
  Phase 2 (owner 2026-10-23, "Eu quero ver o gameplay em tempo real. Faça o patch na engine."), done, not yet on a
  phone: src-patch 0064 mirrors one player's game messages to a cast port on 127.0.0.1 (`castPort`, off by default;
  install step `castport`), `server/tools/cast_relay.py` turns them into JSON lines with sprite sheets drawn on the VPS
  from the real pair, `chaos-ctl.sh cast NAME` streams them over the app's ssh channel and the app draws them at
  about 30 fps (Assistir jogador > Ao vivo; phase 1 stays as Foto). The sprites are still drawn on the VPS, sent once
  per sheet, not read from a pair on the device. server/README.md "Live spectator".
  Phase 3 (O152, 2026-10-23), done, not run on a phone: the other floors the client shows (per-floor offset, the floors
  above hidden when a roof or ceiling covers the player, a floor under a ground not drawn), the world light with
  creature, item and effect light as round glows, and the effects and missiles of other floors. No engine change: the
  cast already carried floors, light, effects and missiles; the relay protocol is version 3 (`"v"` in the hello line,
  new keys only, an older app or relay still works). The relay is unit tested (`cast_relay_test.py`); the app compiles
  against the Android API jar only (no Android SDK on the build machine, so no gradle build) and its arithmetic
  (`CastMath.java`) was run on a JVM; the drawing was never seen on a phone. The floor cover rule and the light curve
  follow the client from memory of its source (the base tree is not in the repo), so they are approximations.
  Still not drawn: the client's own interface.
- Done (monster lint follow-up, server/README.md "Monster lint", `tools/tune_monsters.py`): Wild Boar and Giant
  Hornet renamed Gloomtusk Boar and Blightwing Hornet; donor boss experience in the world boss band; the 2,005,500
  hits and Vharok's dead bleed fixed; late-game gold, Garack's loot, Azure Dragon's elements; Orcaruz, Fenmaw,
  Plagueroot and Mushroom King retuned; the 32 dormant imports clamped to levels 650-1300; four pirate roles.
  Open: computed, not measured in game.
- Decided and done (owner: rename all of it): monsters and items (part one), NPCs, dialogue, quests, raids,
  achievements, map texts and houses (part two), row 88. Every list is keyed by the sha256 of the old name, so the
  repository holds no old name. Left on purpose: file and folder names that players never see (NPC scripts, raid and
  quest folders, storage and event identifiers), and a few item and monster names that are plain English words or the
  old names of NPCs (server/README.md "Base world texts", "Left as they are"). The last coined name of the old world,
  one of our Chaos monsters and the base mount named after it, is now Fenmaw for both (closed): a colossal swamp
  beast that hisses, chokes its prey in bog water and roots it in creepers; the mount is the same beast. The monster's
  file is `monsters/chaos/fenmaw.xml`; the mount follows the hashed row of `base_world_names.csv` (step `worldtexts`,
  mounts.xml and the base mount menu scripts), and the store offer follows `gen_shop_cosmetics.py`.

- Decided: movement speed (engine `server/src-patches/0018-movement-speed.patch`, client
  `client/base-patches/0029-movement-speed.patch`, server/README.md "Movement speed"). Players walk 15-30% faster:
  base +50, 2.5 per level to 300, 1 after; 10 ms steps. A diagonal costs 7/5 of a straight step, was 3x on the
  client. Walk speed is held between 120 and 2600: about 1 to 10 tiles/s on grass. Monsters keep their rules.
  Tested in game at levels 1-1000 with haste and a paralyze rune: no rubber-banding. Open: the Windows client and
  server need rebuilds with these patches. Without 0029 a client predicts slower steps: it stutters, but no
  rubber-banding. The numbers are config keys, so retuning needs no rebuild.
- Fixed: opening the passive tree window with a large build no longer gets players kicked. The window rebuilt
  ~1,400 widgets four times (a 2-4 s freeze); now built once and in slices. Fixed too: the base client's healing
  helper (`mods/game_helper/helper.lua`) replayed every missed 50 ms cycle after any client stall (the engine's
  `cycleEvent` schedules from the last planned time) and flooded spell casts; base patch
  `client/base-patches/0026-helper-no-cycle-replay.patch` gives the helper module a `cycleEvent` that schedules the
  next run from now (after a stall it runs once) and a cast rate guard (same words not within 500 ms, at most 4
  casts a second). Tested in game: 2.5 s stall with healing on, 17 casts in 170 ms before, 1 cast after.
- Fixed: notification toasts (`game_chaosnotify`) had no background: the code loaded `images/background`, the file is
  `Background.png` (file names are case-sensitive on Linux); both the `.otui` and the `.lua` now load `images/Background`.
  (Fixed with item 20: the Daily Reward window no longer opens over other login windows.)
- Done 2026-10-12: the weapon (auto-attack) numbers re-measured with the client's own attack (tree, weapon styles and
  keystones on the dummies; `server/data/spells/README.md` "Line keystones, masteries and weapons measured in game"),
  and the keystones verified in game: Ricochet, Deadeye, Bulwark (visuals), both Illusionist keystones in a party
  ("Keystones verified in game"). Overgrowth and Thorns work when forced on a character but no class could take them
  (the druid variants). The four line keystones and the masteries measured on the dummies.
- Done 2026-10-12 (owner approved; computed, not measured in game): the druid pair has classes again. The Iron Sage may
  take Overgrowth on 621 and the Sentinel Thorns on 624 (existing slots, no layout change, nothing to refund).
  Overgrowth's pulses are plain health, so the healer's bonuses count once (in game 41% instead of 30%), and a new
  heal adds to the running regrowth; 10% for the Iron Sage, since 30% put it ahead of the Illusionist in a group at
  level 1000 (`server/data/spells/README.md` "Overgrowth and Thorns for the Iron Sage and the Sentinel"). Open: measure
  both in game (an Iron Sage healing an ally, a Sentinel holding a pack).
- Done 2026-10-12 (owner approved; computed, not measured in game): the class order breaks of the keystone check.
  Covenant of the Grave's minion hits +20% for the Bone Lord (was +50%; Necromancer and Lich keep +50%), the level 300
  class factor Necromancer 1.0 -> 0.93 and Bone Lord 0.75 -> 0.63 (their path builds to the keystone were level with
  the Deadeye even without it), Blackblood Creed's poison +60% -> +30%. Computed, level 300 / 1000, one target: Bone
  Lord with Covenant 1603 / 5113 -> 1147 / 4327 (area 3186 / 10287 -> 2295 / 8705), Necromancer 1246 -> 1156 at level
  300, Ninja with the Creed 4566 -> 4374 at level 1000; the Deadeye (1258 / 4733) 8-10% ahead of each.
  `server/data/spells/README.md` "Class order fixes". Measured in game 2026-10-13 (two samples each): Bone Lord with
  Covenant 1162, 1177 / 4190, 4227 (area 2318 / 8303, 8159), Necromancer 1158, 1150 / 2970, 2952, Ninja with the Creed
  1083, 1123 / 4327, 3948; the Deadeye (1271 / 4670-4685 on the same server) 8.6-15% ahead of each, the order holds.
  Flags: the Thief (2578, -10.3%) and the Samurai (2218, -14.7%) with the Creed at level 1000 are under their
  computed rows; no change proposed (far behind the leaders).
- Fixed 2026-10-12: the combat log's `doTargetCombat` wrapper turned a left-out origin into `ORIGIN_NONE`, so since
  2026-10-10 every `ChaosSpell.damageTarget` / `healTarget` hit and heal ran no health events (no tree, class factor,
  keystones, Weaver's Grace or PvP healing rule): the Deadeye was 20-25% low. Measured before / after in
  `server/data/spells/README.md` "The combat log wrapper and the balance". The Mystic at level 1000 (1701-1824 against
  2439 in the C4 table) was the test kit: the character had lost levels (245 tree points instead of 248), so the kit
  gave it the level 300 wand; its build was the C4 one (the log left out fire). Harness fixed, no game change
  ("Class order fixes"). Measured again 2026-10-13 with the fixed kit: the Mystic at level 1000 2455 / 2605 (C4 2439).
  Still open: the two healers' numbers on an ally with the fix.
- Fixed: the base rarity element script (`data/scripts/customs/rarity/rarity_onCombat_elementdamage.lua`) returned
  nothing when a player wore two different rarity elements, so the engine dealt 0 damage. `patch_base_events.py`
  (step 9) now hands that case to `chaos/rarity_elements.lua`: primary kept, each element its own share (one in the
  secondary, the others as their own hits right after); the mana handler counts every element.
  `tests/rarity_elements_test.lua`; tested in game (0 damage before, fire + ice shares after).
- Fixed: `/testkit` (`admin/testkit.lua`) called `addHealth(n)`, which does nothing on this engine (it takes a
  damage type first), so health was not refilled. All one-argument calls now go through `Chaos.heal(creature, amount)`
  (chaos_constants.lua): the world boss adds and Vharok's heal, the Infinity Tower's heal between floors, the
  Regenerating / Vampiric monster modifiers, the admin panel's Heal; the spells (Wild Bond, Crimson Covenant's health
  cost) pass the damage type themselves. Fixed too: free accounts no longer lose their ascended class at login
  (`patch_base_events.py`, step 4 of its list).
- Note: the engine only runs `onTargetCombat` for weapon hits after the player clicks to attack; targets set
  from Lua skip it, and with it the rarity, passive tree and combat feedback damage events.
- The Windows server binary has no `server/src-patches/` applied: no "CRIT!" visual there until it is rebuilt.
- Illusionist: decided, a support class (party attack buffs and some healing), the Mystic's second ascension.
- Community (Discord) invite link: `ChaosTopBar.COMMUNITY_URL` in `client/overlay/modules/client_chaos_topbar/chaos_topbar.lua` is empty until there is one.
- Done: the client's xBRZ is 1.8 (was 1.4), `client/base-patches/0027-xbrz-1.8.patch`, GPL-3 as before. 1.8 reads outside the
  image as transparent, which cut notches and holes into the seams of multi-sprite outfits, so each sprite is padded with
  its edge pixels first; it also drops the 64 MB colour table (and a ~0.1 s stall on the first sprite). Compared with 1.4
  at x2/x3/x4 in the Linux client and over 50000 sprites: nearly the same picture (under 0.15% of pixels differ, slightly
  more consistent edges), same speed (40-110 us per sprite). Windows build not run.
- The base server's own world-boss timer (`data/scripts/customs/worldbosses/`) stays next to the Chaos world bosses: nine weekly open-world island bosses with their own loot, at other times (the closest: 20:00 vs the Chaos 21:00), places, monsters and rewards than the Chaos arena event, no shared board or broadcast; why, and how to remove it if wanted: server/README.md "World bosses".
- Absorb ceiling: decided 2026-10-20, 75% per element. Engine patch `0049-absorb-ceiling` written and dry-run applied, **not compiled or run in game**. Still to do: `maxAbsorb` 80 -> 75 in `passivetree_effects.lua` and the matching texts/tests (server/README.md "Absorb ceiling"); the base Lua absorb event still sums the storages uncapped.

Cause 2 (confirmed in code): that list's boss entry and the map record's boss were both made, two bosses; `fillFromMap` made every record entry, the boss too. Cause 3 (confirmed in code): the boss was made without a level, so `monster_levels.lua` showed the estimate of its type (2, 8); health and damage are not read from it (display only, O177), the boss curve `bossHealth` applied as before. Also: every placement was forced (`createMonster` force), which puts a monster on a wall or on another one. Now (`dungeons.lua`): the base list is only the fallback of a dungeon without a start-up record; every monster is placed unforced on a walkable free tile (`Tile` ground, no `TILESTATE_BLOCKSOLID`, one per tile, inside the area) or the nearest one within `Chaos.dungeons.placement.radius` (4), else not made and not counted; what already stands there is settled the same way (`settleMonsters`); the boss is never part of the entry roster, `placeBoss` makes it once (`run.bossSpawned`) with the dungeon's level, a second boss is removed; the unlock (`extra.unlock`) is asked once (`fireUnlock`) also when the stall unlock and the 85% meet. Tests: `tests/dungeons_area_test.lua` block O183; server/README.md "dungeons" |

It does not read the server's spell type, so the O184 / O185 mis-typing is not its cause. `game_helperrotation/autosetup_classes_test.lua` runs it for all 21 classes on the client's own spell data (spelldata.lua, stock_spells.csv): every class gets 5 distinct attack spells of its class at level 2000 (also with a client that folds the ascension ids), every attack spell of the kit is in a preset or the extras (except Snare Trap, Seeker Mine, Corpse Explosion, flagged by hand in rotation_model.csv), and level 150 holds what the class can cast. Next step: the log line `[auto rotation]` of a Ninja after Auto setup.. 2026-10-27: checked every level 1 to 300 for all 21 classes (`autosetup_classes_test.lua`): the rotation always holds min(castable attack spells not cast by hand, 5), every preset spell is castable at the preset's own level, and the client's vocation byte 16 maps to server id 12 (base patch 0016). Other suspects not excluded: a stale cache in `game_helper/helper.lua` `buildSpellCache` (one entry per spell id, the last wins) when a stock client id collides with a Chaos id in the 236 to 243 range where Ninja and Thief spells sit, and the learned list `player:getSpells()`. `canCastFor` now uses `ChaosSpellFix.allowsVocation` (the rule of every other picker) and names why a spell was refused (server, class, level, learned); `Auto rotation` writes one `[auto rotation] vocation V level L picked N, refused id:reason ...` line into the client log. Next step: that line from the owner's Ninja; the tester check `auto_setup` also holds every bot's preset against the server spells |
| O187 | Owner beta 2026-10-24: "In the Ninja's Merciless trial, the Marked Cutthroat keeps reviving, so it is impossible to count the kills" | done (mocked-engine tests under luajit and lua5.3; not run live, the cause is the code path shown, not reproduced on a server; server: `files` step and a restart) | Checked and ruled out: the thralls are skipped by `monster_tiers.lua` (no rare modifier, no ambush copy: `skipTiers()` in `trials.lua`), they have no map spawn (chamber grid x/y 9000+), no modifier revives, the necromancer `rise` mechanic is the only intended revival. Cause (confirmed in code, `trials.lua` `tickMarks`): a mark was removed after `markSeconds` (16 / 13 / 10) whether or not it was being fought, "comes back" at full health (`markIdx - 1`, `spawnMark`) and costs 20 s, with no limit. Its health is a share of the boss health (Merciless level 400: 7020 health in 10 s = 700 damage a second), so a Ninja dealing less never killed one: it kept vanishing and returning and the count stayed. Now: `M.markStep` (`trials_core.lua`): every tick that finds the mark lower starts its clock again, an untouched mark flees at most `markMaxFlees` (3) times per run and then stays until it dies; a failed spawn no longer burns a mark number; a mark that vanishes without a death event counts as a kill and `M.stageKills` fills the counter when the stage ends; risen thralls (necromancer) are not counted twice. Safety nets: `ChaosTrials.runAt(pos)` / `isTrialMonster`: no ambush copy (also one queued before the trial opened), no tier and no Treasure Goblin in a running chamber, the map respawn is refused there like in a dungeon. Tests: `tests/trials_test.lua` (block O187, reproduces the old rule), `trials_flow_test.lua`, `monster_tiers_test.lua`. |

| O196 | Server hardening: systemd sandbox, no secret core dumps, SQL integer guards (no gameplay change; deploys through the live VPS) | done (unit test in CI, `systemd-analyze verify` and `security --offline` run here; live not run here: the units are not installed by `update-apply`, the owner copies them once) | `chaos-server.service`: `LimitCORE=0` (nothing in the repo uses cores, they hold the DB password and the keys; a one-core recipe with root-only capped systemd-coredump is in the docs), sandbox lines (private devices, kernel / clock / hostname protection, no namespaces, realtime, SUID, personality, native syscalls only, empty capability set, `ProtectProc=invisible`, address families INET / INET6 / UNIX / NETLINK), `ProtectSystem=full` kept on purpose (the base engine's writes cannot be listed, the Lua writes can), no `UMask=` (website and `chaos-ctl` read the files), no `MemoryDenyWriteExecute` / `SystemCallFilter`. The root `ExecStopPost` now skips `chaos_crashlog.sh` (one journal line) when the script or its directories belong to `chaos` or are group / world writable, or `find` fails. Other units: `LimitCORE=0` everywhere, the same sandbox for the four unprivileged ones, a safe subset for the root ones and the kit unit. Exposure score 8.0 -> 3.0 for the game server. SQL: `ChaosAdmin.sqlInt` in the ban / unban / account actions, `sqlInt` and an amount check in `bank.lua` transfers; `tests/sql_key_guard_test.lua` feeds the `validKey` / `cleanVersion` guards hostile strings. Tests: `server/deploy/chaos_units_test.py`, `tests/bank_test.lua`, `tests/admin_panel_test.lua`, `tests/sql_key_guard_test.lua` (luajit and lua5.3). | O190 | Owner beta 2026-10-24: "The skill tree keeps resetting when the player dies. It happened to the Ninja." | done (tests under luajit and lua5.3; not run live; server: `files` step and a restart) | Cause (confirmed by reproduction, not class specific): the logins emptied a whole allocation when it held more than the points of the new level. `passivetree.lua` `refundOverBudget` ran `UPDATE ... SET nodes = ''` on every preset with more nodes than `totalPoints`, and `paragon.lua` login saved an empty allocation when `spent > total`. A death takes levels; a character that spent all its points (one per 4 levels, Paragon one per level to 1100, then one per 6) lost its whole tree at the next login after a single death that crossed a point threshold. The Ninja was only the one who reported it. Fix: an allocation over budget is kept; `ChaosPassiveTreeCore.debt` / `ChaosParagon.debt` only tell the player how many nodes or points are over, spending waits for the level, nothing is written at login. `refundForeignNodes` does nothing when the vocation is not readable. Tests: `tests/passivetree_death_test.lua` (all 21 classes, 10 level bands around the thresholds and past 1100, 8 losses, the Thief tree on a Ninja, transient states; fails on the old code with the UPDATE), block O190 in `tests/paragon_test.lua`. Allocations already wiped are not stored anywhere and cannot be restored by the server (saved loadouts and database backups are the only copies). |

Only whole steps because the fonts are bitmaps (a stretch by 1.5 is uneven). A step the window is too small for is lowered (1080p stays 100 %, 1440p up to 200 %, 4K up to 300 %). | O208 | Weather on the client | done in code, untested in a real client and on a live server (server logic unit tested under luajit and Lua 5.3 on a mocked engine, `tests/weather_test.lua`; the config patch tool tested on a copy of the stock text and run through `install_all.py --only baserain`; client logic unit tested, `chaosweather_test.lua`; the art generator `--check`; base patch 0130 (Lua and otui only) assembled after 0001-0129 with every hunk applied without fuzz or offset; checked by reading the base sources: the base rain script, the base client's native `WeatherManager`, `UIWidget::drawImage`; the widgets and the opcode were not run, the picture was only rendered to a PNG by hand) | The base server's rain (`customs/globalevents/rain.lua`) sent a map effect and a distance effect to every player every 100 ms while it rained (6 s of game loop and 57 MB of garbage a minute at 200 online, server/README.md "Game loop stalls"). Now the server only decides: `weather.lua` keeps the base's dice (15% to start, 10% to stop, every 5 minutes), picks a strength 1-3 per rain and, every 3 s, sends a player one small JSON message on extended opcode 114 when it changes for him (login, `hello`, crossing between the surface floors 0-7 and underground, a new roll). Per hour of steady rain at 200 online that is 200 messages instead of about 14 million packets. The base's switch `weatherRain = false` (`patch_base_rain.py`, step `baserain`) turns its script off without editing it. The client module `game_chaosweather` draws the rain with three repeated 256 px pictures on widgets over the game map only (not over panels), scrolled by `setImageRect` every 33 ms, 3 layers at most, 126 rectangles at 1080p and 561 at 4K, fading in and out, hidden under a roof (`g_map.isCovered`) and by the option `weather` (Options > Graphics, base patch `0130`, game_visualfx). No C++: the base client's native `WeatherManager` (256 particles, clipped to the map) exists but only a game-protocol packet drives it and Lua has no binding, so using it would need a client and a server engine patch. Dropped on purpose: the base's thunder (1-5 energy damage about once in 30 s out in the rain). Not the same as before: the base drew a few splashes and streaks at a time, this draws a denser, steady curtain; level 2 is the nearest to it. Open: run it in a client and look at it (density, speed, colours, the roof check next to buildings, the fade), measure the frame cost at 4K, tune `rain_*.png` and `L.PLAN`; snow, sand and wind areas would be new `areas` kinds in `weather_config.lua` |
| O209 | Lighter game connection: packet compression | done in code, off by default, untested in a real client and on a live server (server patch `0069` and client base patch `0131` applied after all earlier ones with 0 fuzz / FAILED; the engine files and `protocol.cpp` compiled with `-fsyntax-only` only, no full build or link; `Deflater` round trip against the client's inflate method tested, `tools/packet_compress_test.cpp`; bandwidth and CPU measured offline with `tools/packet_compress_bench.py` on packets built from the real tile data of the base map, not on a live capture) | The client engine already inflates packets whose checksum word is 0 (one raw deflate stream per connection, sync flush). The server now sends them: bodies of 128 to 60000 bytes, level 3, only to our client (its login markers), only with `packetCompression = true` in `config.lua` (default false). Server to client only, so nothing the server reads is inflated; XTEA stays outermost. Saved on the wire: 81% (real map) on a login view, 70% on a step, 76% on module JSON, 26% on fight ticks, about 36% on a mixed hunt minute; about 12 us of CPU per compressed packet (about 7% of one core at 200 hunting players, estimated) and 128 KB of memory per connection. Base patch `0131` caps one inflated packet at 65536 bytes and closes the connection on overflow instead of cutting it short. Not done: a capability bit (the login block is full), a fix for a plain packet whose adler is 0 (1 in 4 billion, as before), a live capture to replace the generated fight and JSON traffic, a CPU measurement on the live server, a CI entry for the bench and the C++ test. Open: turn it on on a test server, watch the tick time (`slowTaskLogMs`), then decide on `packetCompressionMin` 64 (50% on the mixed minute, twice the CPU). Review fixes: server patch `0073` (a block that did not fit the message drops the deflater instead of desyncing the stream; compression starts after the password check, not for unauthenticated connections) and client base patch `0135` (zlib footer bound counts the header offset, overflow disconnects); syntax-checked only, not built or run. See server/README.md "Packet compression (O209)" |
| O210 | Client: soft glow (bloom) on bright light only | done in code, off by default, untested in a real client (base patch `0132` applied after all earlier ones with 0 fuzz / FAILED; the touched engine files compiled with `g++ -fsyntax-only` only, no full build or link; the two shaders compile as GLSL 1.10 and ES 1.00, `check_shaders.py`; the option logic is tested, `visualfx_test.lua`; no frame was rendered, so the look and the cost are paper numbers) | The owner's rule (`docs/MAP_GUIDE.md` "The owner's taste"): the biome's own colours stay. Option `bloom` "Soft glow" in Options > Graphics, default off. Only pixels of the finished map picture with a luma above 0.80 (full weight at 0.96) feed the glow, at 1/4 size, blurred once each way, and the result is screen-blended over the map (src + dst * (1 - src)) at strength 0.35: black adds nothing, white cannot get whiter, so a pixel far from a bright one is not changed at all and no tint or wash-out is possible by construction. Cost on, in fill rate: about 0.19 screen pixels shaded and 0.9 screen-pixel texture reads for the small passes, plus one full-map-rect blend (1 pixel, 1 read); off: one atomic read, no buffers. Known: bright ground (snow, white sand, a bright floor under full light) passes the threshold and gets a faint halo at its edges next to darker ground; the halo is drawn after the vignette grade, so it is not darkened at the corners. Open: look at it in a client (strength 0.35, threshold 0.80, ramp to 0.96), measure the frame time on a weak GPU, decide the default. See client/BASE.md patch 69y, client/MANIFEST.md "Visual effects (O203)" |
| O211 | Server: cheaper monster pathfinding, measured | done in code, not run on a live server (offline benchmark and unit test only; the engine was not built or linked; the patch chain 0001-0070 applies to the base source with 0 FAILED / 0 fuzz and the touched engine files pass `g++ -fsyntax-only`) | Audit of `Map::getPathMatching`, `AStarNodes`, `Creature::onThink` / `goToFollowCreature` and the monster walk code. Finding: a monster searches at most once per 1 s think (about 0.5-0.9 searches per monster per second in the benchmark), so a repath throttle has nothing to cut; the cost is the stock node store (12.8 KB zeroed per search, one heap allocation per node, a scan of all nodes per expansion). Engine patch 0070 (`src/chaospathing.h`): `ChaosPath::NodeStore` with a reused store, open addressing table and a binary heap ordered (f, index), which pops the same node as the old scan, so every path is the same. Measured on windows of the real map (`tools/path_bench.cpp`): one search 19.8 -> 13.2 us (target reachable, 1.5x), 48.5 -> 29.4 us (unreachable, 1.65x); a pack of 12 or 40 monsters chasing: search time 1.65x-1.95x less, identical searches, nodes and distances. About 1.6% -> 0.8% of one core at 500 hunting monsters, estimated from the benchmark; the live share is smaller because the engine's tile tests are costlier than the grid lookups here. A search memo (skip repeated searches for an unmoved target) was measured and not shipped: 3-20% fewer searches and a pack that reroutes later. Tests: `tools/chaospathing_test.cpp` (16000 random searches old against new store, ASan/UBSan), `tools/path_bench.cpp --check` (CI "Native checks"). No patch note (players do not see it). Open: build the engine, compare the tick time with a big pack on a test server, decide on a negative cache if the search shows in a profile. See server/README.md "Cheaper monster pathfinding (O211)" |
| O212 | Server: cheaper spectator queries, measured | done in code, not run on a live server (offline benchmark and unit test only; the engine was not built or linked; the patch chain 0001-0071 applies to the base source with 0 FAILED / 0 fuzz and the touched engine files pass `g++ -fsyntax-only`) | Audit of `Map::getSpectators` and its callers (`Map::moveCreature`, `addMagicEffect`, `addDistanceEffect`, combat areas, health bars). Finding: the sector walk is cheap (about 180 ns for 45 creatures); the stock cost around it was memory: `SpectatorVec` reserved 32 on the heap in its constructor (an allocation per caller), the `std::map` cache allocated a node and a vector per store and was freed again at the next creature step (every step clears it), `addSpectators` was n * m, and `moveCreature` grew a vector one push_back at a time. Engine patch 0071 (`src/chaosspectators.h`): `SpectatorVec` on a small vector with 48 inline slots, a generation stamped cache over reused vectors (clear in O(1)), a hashed union in the same order, stack positions on a small vector. Same creatures in the same order: `tools/spectator_bench.cpp --check` runs the stock and the new code on the same random operations and compares every answer. Measured (45 creatures in a screen; 100 players in a city; 260 in a crowd): per tick of steps, a spell and effects 69.7 -> 48.7 us (hunt, 1.43x), 114.2 -> 80.4 us (city, 1.42x), 2471 -> 1107 us (crowd, 2.23x); allocations 590 -> 1 per hunt tick. A saving of about 30% of the spectator code in a hunt or city, 55% in a crowd; in absolute terms small (the spectator code is about 0.14% of one core at 500 hunting monsters, the patch takes about 0.4 ms per second of that). Not done: skipping the cache clear when nothing in range changed (the leaf lists remove by swap, so a kept entry could differ in order from a fresh walk; and in a pack everything is in range); one union query for a step (changes the notify order). No patch note (players do not see it). Tests: `tools/chaosspectators_test.cpp` (against std containers, ASan/UBSan), `tools/spectator_bench.cpp --check` (CI "Native checks"). Open: build the engine, compare the tick time with a big pack or a crowd on a test server. See server/README.md "Cheaper spectator queries (O212)" |
| O213 | Server: cheaper map description, measured | done in code, not run on a live server (offline benchmark and unit test only; the engine was not built or linked, and `protocolgame.cpp` / `map.cpp` were not even syntax-checked here: no Lua or Boost headers; the patch chain 0001-0072 applies to the base source with 0 FAILED / 0 fuzz) | Audit of the map description (`ProtocolGame::GetMapDescription`, `GetFloorDescription`, `GetTileDescription`, the floor change paths, the known creature list, `NetworkMessage`). Finding: `GetFloorDescription` called `Map::getTile` for every tile, a 12 hop quad tree walk each, although 64 neighbouring tiles share one 8 x 8 leaf and floor (about 2000 walks for a full description). Engine patch 0072 (`src/chaosmapdesc.h`): the same loop and skip counter with the `Floor` resolved once per block (four slots), 9 walks instead of 252 per floor; `Map::getFloorAt`. The bytes are identical: `tools/chaosmapdesc_test.cpp` compares a copy of the stock loop on 60 synthetic maps (edges, missing floors, runs over 254 empty tiles), `tools/mapdesc_bench.cpp --check` compares every byte it times and fails below 1.15x. Measured with a light stand-in tile writer: full description 97 -> 67 us (city, 1.44x), 99 -> 75 us (cave, 1.32x), step row 1.29x, step column 1.21-1.28x, sea 2.29x; in the engine the share saved is smaller, the tile writer (creatures, outfits) is unchanged. Left alone: the known creature list (eviction order follows the `unordered_set` iteration, a new container could change the bytes) and `NetworkMessage` (patch 0032 did it). Open: build the engine and time a login, a teleport and steps on a test server. See server/README.md "Cheaper map description (O213)" |
| O214 | Client: cheaper per-frame names, bars and damage numbers, measured | done in code, offline bench only, untested in a real client (the engine was not built or linked; the touched `.cpp` files pass `g++ -fsyntax-only`; the patch chain 0001-0133 applies to the base source with 0 FAILED / 0 fuzz); goes live with the next client build | Audit of the text path (`Creature::drawInformation`, `CachedText`, `StaticText`, `AnimatedText`, `BitmapFont`, `TextRender`, `DrawQueue`). Finding: the glyph layout was already cached per string by `TextRender`; the waste was finding it again on every draw (hash of the whole string, mutex and `std::map` search in `addText`, then mutex, search and a clock read in the draw). Engine patch 0133 (`chaostextslot.h`): `CachedText` keeps its layout entry in a `TextSlot` keyed by font, font texture, box size, alignment and a text revision, one entry per text, rebuilt only on a change; colour and scale are not in a layout. Same coordinates: `client/tools/chaostextslot_test.cpp` checks the entry in use against the stock layout after 34795 random draws and changes (also under ASan/UBSan), `client/tools/text_bench.cpp --check` compares every quad it times and fails below 1.15x. Measured (lookup, queue item and stand-in painter only): 60 names 5.8 -> 3.1 us per frame (1.85x), plus 15 damage numbers 8.0 -> 3.9 us (2.04x). The OpenGL work and the rest of `drawInformation` are unchanged, so the gain in a real frame is a small part of that. Open: build the client and time a busy hunt screen (profiler on `drawInformation` and the text queue). See client/BASE.md 0133 |

| O223 | Client: short fades on main windows and dialogs | done in code, untested in a real client (logic unit tested under luajit and Lua 5.3, `uifade_test.lua`; the hook run against a mocked widget API, `uifade_widget_test.lua`: open, close, turn round, destroy mid-fade, option off, terminate; base patch `0141` (Lua and OTUI only) applied after all earlier ones with 0 FAILED / fuzz; no engine code, nothing built or run) | Audit: the base has `g_effects.fadeIn / fadeOut` (30 ms steps, no clock, hide-on-finish resets opacity to a wrong value) and `UIWidget:setOpacity`, which the draw queue applies to a widget and all its children; no engine change needed. The new module `client_chaos_uifade` hooks the `UIWindow` class once: `onVisibilityChange(true)` and `UIWindow.create` (windows the loader makes visible) fade in (140 ms, ease-out), `hide()` fades out (100 ms) and hides for real at the end, so no window module is edited. While closing the window is phantom (clicks pass through), has given its focus on, and Lua's `isVisible()` already says hidden; `show()` during a close turns round from the current opacity. Every timer step checks that the widget is alive and that its fade is still current; every other hide path (`setVisible(false)`, parent hidden), a destroy and terminate end at opacity 1 with the phantom flag as before. Covered: `UIWindow` instances (MainWindow, NewMainWindow, dialogs) at least 140x80, child of the root or of `gameRootPanel`, closed with `hide()`; open on any show. Not covered (instant): `destroy()` (a ghost would keep a dead window's id), `UIMiniWindow` side panels, phantom windows (tooltips), small popups, windows with `chaosNoFade = true`. No scale or slide: the engine has no widget scale and a slide would break `centerIn` anchors. Option `uiFade` (Options > Graphics "Fade windows in and out", default on, panel 257 -> 276 px). Cost: a 16 ms timer per window for 100-140 ms. | O225 | Server: cheaper walk map refresh of walking monsters, measured | done in code, not run on a live server (offline benchmark and unit test only; the engine was not built or linked; `creature.cpp` passes `g++ -fsyntax-only` on the patched base source; the patch chain 0001-0076 applies with 0 FAILED, 0076 with 0 fuzz) | Fifth engine hot path after O211-O213 and O218. Audit of the monster think and target code by reading. `Monster::searchTarget`'s `std::list` of candidates was tried (stack array, identical choices) and dropped: only 1.04-1.12x of a search against the engine's own attack test, the sight lines are the cost. `updateTargetList`'s `updateIdleStatus` per spectator is not redundant (an empty target list sets the monster idle and clears its friends), left alone. Found instead: every step of a walking monster fills the new edge of its 33 x 25 walk grid (`Creature::onCreatureMove`, a row of 33 or a column of 25 tiles) with `Map::getTile` per tile, a quad tree walk each; `updateMapCache` does 825. Engine patch 0076 (`src/chaosstrip.h`): the same tile through a reader that keeps the `Floor` of the current 8 x 8 block on the stack of one refresh, 4-5 walks instead of 25-33; no allocation, nothing kept between calls. Tiles identical: `tools/chaosstrip_test.cpp` compares `Map::getTile` on 3.5 million reads (edges, wrap at 16 bits, missing floors; ASan too), `tools/strip_bench.cpp --check` compares every cache it times and fails below 1.15x. Measured with a light stand-in tile test: a step 1.8 -> 0.97 us (row) and 0.93 -> 0.55 us (column), whole grid 33 -> 18 us, 1.7-1.9x; in the engine `queryAdd` is unchanged, so the share saved is smaller. Open: build the engine and profile a busy hunt. See server/README.md "Cheaper walk map refresh (O225)" |

Monsters that stay drawn after death: not reproduced, causes listed for a phone log, and `game_visualfx` switches 8 heavy effect options off once on the first Android start; neither run on a device. Loading screen 2026-10-30: client base patch `0168` and `ChaosActivity` show the logo, "Loading..." and a progress bar until the first frame, so the long first load is no longer a black screen. Screen fit 2026-10-30: module `client_chaos_screenfit` makes any window taller or wider than the screen scroll by drag or wheel, opening with its title in view (owner chose this over a new UI library; a test window in another UI library comes next). Plan: small APK, game data on first start through the signed manifest (an `android` section like O314's `native`), the APK updated by a signed versioned download through the package installer (sideload first, store later), 11 build steps, about 4 to 6 weeks to a client that starts. Phase 2 done 2026-10-29: client base patches `0163` (CMake build for android arm64 with the NDK, plus the compile and link fixes it needed) and `0164` (JNI class name), a vcpkg manifest and triplet, a Gradle project `client/android/` (package `com.cindersofchaos.client`, minSdk 26, arm64 only, 16 KB aligned) and the manual workflow `.github/workflows/android-client.yml` (debug-signed APK as an artifact; release key from the secrets `ANDROID_CLIENT_KEYSTORE_B64`, `ANDROID_CLIENT_KEYSTORE_PASS` and `ANDROID_CLIENT_KEY_ALIAS`). Checked here: every patch applies in order on a fresh base, every engine source compiles with NDK r27d, an APK is built and passes `aapt2`, `zipalign` and `apksigner`. The manifest payload gets the key `android` (the files a phone needs: the shard set, the dat and the rest of the update, minus `layouts/mobile`; `make_update_manifest.py build` writes it on every publish, `--android-exclude` and `--no-android`; old clients ignore it), client base patch `0167` (`g_resources.unpackUpdateBlob`, a file-backed PhysFS mount of the downloaded folder in front of `data.zip`, `getFreeSpace`, the Android restart request) and the module `client_chaos_firststart` (first-start screen: size, Wi-Fi advice, free-space check of data plus 0.5 GB, pause, resume, retry, then a restart; the signature, paths and download pool are the desktop updater's). Chosen default: plain verified files in the app's private folder, no AES blobs on the phone. The Java shell restarts the game through a helper activity (`RestartActivity`). `android-client.yml` takes `update_manifest` and `update_public_key` with the production defaults and its smoke test asserts the first-start screen in logcat. Owner step: the live manifest has no `android` section until the next publish with the new publisher (Painel > Atualizar servidor, then Comandos > Site e update do client > Publicar update with a newer `winpkg-N`). | O314 | Owner 2026-10-29 (pt-BR): "vira e mexe eu preciso atualizar [o client] baixando de novo no site ... seria melhor a gente fazer um launcher?" | done in code and tests, not run on Windows | Cause: the signed updater (client/MANIFEST.md "Signed updater") never downloads native code by design; every release with a new engine patch (client/base-patches, 9 commits since 2026-10-15) sets `min_package` and the client asks for a full download from the website. Plan: a small launcher (Rust, sharing the updater crate of the desktop admin app O312) that runs before the game, so nothing is locked: fetches the same Ed25519-signed manifest, downloads the changed files including the exe and DLLs, verifies every hash, swaps them, starts the game; updates itself by a staged rename at its next start; keeps the in-game Lua updater for the module-only updates. Done 2026-10-29 (`launcher/`, `crates/selfupdate/` shared with `desktop/`, `client/MANIFEST.md` "Game launcher", `launcher/README.md` release steps): Rust launcher with an egui window (pt-BR and en, Jogar), the system `curl`, the same signed manifest and key; a new `native` section of the signed payload (old clients ignore it, `files` unchanged) built by `make_update_manifest.py --native-dir` and added by `publish_client_update.sh` when the zip holds the launcher; staged download with resume, hash checks, journaled swap with rollback and crash recovery, self-update by a staged rename; `windows-package.yml` builds it and the separate client download starts with `CindersLauncher.exe` + `launcher.json`. Tests: `launcher/tests/mass_update.rs` (120 rounds against a local HTTP server, cut download, bad hash, bad signature, rollback, self-update), `vector.rs`, Python `Native` tests; CI step "Game launcher (O314)". Open: a code-signing certificate against antivirus warnings, the install path (the folder needs write rights; a per-user installer is not built), a run on Windows (swap of a loaded exe and DLL, `curl.exe`), no delta for the 0.9 GB `chaos.pack`. | O326 | Owner 2026-10-29 (pt-BR): monsters with a skull are still too strong, remove the skull mechanic | done in code and tests, not run live | **Cause:** `Monster::Monster` rolled a level uniformly in the `<level>` range of a level-scaled type (our 140 XML files and 210 base Lua types), added health (0.0074), speed (0.0085) and damage (0.0015) per level and set a skull colour by level (50-100 white up to 401+ black); two monsters of a type differed by up to +296% health at level 400, the skull marked the high rolls. **Fix:** engine patch `0082-monster-level-no-skull-no-spread.patch`: no level skull (XML `<skull>` and `creature:setSkull` stay), and the level is the middle of the range, so a monster of a type has exactly the old mean strength and the spread is gone. No config or data change, the models and scaling scripts that assume `1 + 0.0074 x level` stay right (now exactly). Test `monster_level_patch_test.py` (CI). server/README.md "Monster level: no skull, no spread (O326)". **Open:** the engine must be rebuilt (patch 0082); the `[level]` after a name is now the same for a whole type; Lua-driven damage is scaled as before |
| O327 | Owner 2026-10-29 (pt-BR): a rare Rootling Nutshell above the ascension level does not count | done in code and tests, not run live | **Cause:** the Proof of Strength recomputed the monster level (bounty pool, area point) instead of the level on the nameplate, read the tier after `ChaosTierDeath` had cleared it, and credited only the last-hit and most-damage killers. **Fix:** `ascension_quest.lua` judges the display level (`getDisplayLevel`) against the bar the tracker shows, falls back to `ChaosTierDead`, and credits the killer, every damager and the killer's party members within `proof.partyRange` once per monster, each against their own bar. Test `ascension_quest_test.lua`. server/README.md "Ascension Proof, nameplate level". Script reload is enough |
| O328 | Tester 2026-10-29: Infinity Tower monsters shown at level ~1200 hit like their level 100 version, drop the loot of the original species, and the tower is only good for AFK orbs | done in code and tests, not run live | **Damage:** the floor's `damage` factor is now the pace damage curve at the shown level L times `hit`(L) over `hit` at the band's hunting level (`infinitytower_balance_config.lua` `hit`: the stock hit of a monster by level), so a monster shown at level L hits like a hunt monster of level L (`tower_model.py --derive`, `--damage` prints before and after; `--check` bounds every floor within 12%). Bosses unchanged up to floor 120. **Loot:** a tower monster counts at its floor's level in `loot.lua` (`ChaosTowerRun.lootLevelOf`, capped at the level of floor 120): item level, gear by level, class weapons, rarity, orb and unique gates. Its corpse is thinned with the pace factors of that level x `lootShare` (`lootFactorsOf`), so the items an hour of the tower stay at a hunt of the same level, and each corpse holds `killGold` in coins (goldShare 0.5 of a hunt's gold an hour); the floor gold of a floor above 120 is that of 120. Both curves are `--derive` lines and `--check` bounds items and gold an hour per floor (`lootRatio`, `goldRatio`). **XP:** not changed: the tower pays 0.03-0.35 of a hunt's experience an hour at the same level (stock species experience, unthinned, pre-stage), so it is not the source of the 750 in 7 h report. Tests `infinitytower_balance_test.lua`, `infinitytower_run_test.lua`, `loot_tower_test.lua`, `tower_model_test.py`. server/README.md "Infinity Tower: damage and loot follow the floor (O328)". **Open:** the `hit` reference below level 300 is an estimate from stock species, tune it on the live server; computed, not measured in game; floor 1 monsters hit about 7x their stock (a level 100 hunt monster hits like that) |

Now the owner (else the first party member who may) standing on the tile takes it at once (`K.firstTaker`), and a sweep in the look-out removes any item of ours whose record is gone (`K.track` / `K.strays`, every tile an item was put on) and every copy of it on the tile. Tests: `tests/globes_test.lua`. Server reload only. **Dead monster (3):** `game_deadguard` (earlier today) only reacts to a health event of 0; it cannot see a monster that was at 0 before it started, nor a second object of one creature id (the refresh of server src-patch 0083 made one; the later removal takes the object the map knows, the other stays, keeps its old health, is clickable and the helper cannot attack it). Added a once-a-second sweep: a monster seen at 0 percent for 3 s, and the non-canonical object of a duplicated id (only when exactly one of the objects is `g_map.getCreatureById(id)`), removed locally with one log line each. Needs a client update. Check src-patch 0083 is built into the live server. **Invisible (1):** nothing we ship makes a player invisible by taking a pickup (the ember pool is XP, gold, damage, ward, speed, regen, crit, loot, rarity, orbs; the globes heal). Only Shadow Veil (4 s) and Wraithshroud (8 s) and the admin toggle set CONDITION_INVISIBLE; the engine then sends the creature's outfit as blank, so an invisible player is not drawn while standing. **Update (owner: the invisibility always comes with the ghost monster):** one shared cause, a stack position that missed on a client tile. Client `drawCreatures` draws a standing creature from the tile's things and a walking one from the tile's walking list, so a character removed from the things (by a 0x6B or 0x6C packet whose index hit it) is invisible standing and visible walking; a ghost monster is the same miss the other way (the removal hit another thing). Trigger not proven: no pickup or class code changes an outfit. Suspects: a tile where server and client disagree on the stack (item wire bytes, `item_wire.csv`; ember and globe nodes copied from stock nodes with the client look of pair 55; a stackable merge sends a 0x6B by index), src-patch 0083 not built on the live server. Other bases that fit: 2379 (rejected: the starter kit and the off-hand grant count that id) or a base of its own that keeps the old look (more install steps). **Old copies** turn into 7408 when moved and at login, a worn one through the backpack with a message (`chaos_jewelry.lua` `uniqueMoves`, the path of the jewelry uniques); a plain copy of 27193 stays a wand. **Install.** Step `uniqueequip` puts the stock Equip entry of a base that left the list back (27193: level 180, the two caster lines); before, a re-run would have dropped it. **Every other unique.** 52 unique weapons on stock bases compared with `base_weapons.csv` and with the weapons.xml of the installed folder: no other fails. `tests/unique_weapon_base_test.lua` holds kind, classes and level from now on. **Harvester of Sorrows:** the test failed when O500 came in, which typed the scythes as swords while this unique on 10301 still said axe; it says sword now (`weaponType`, `skillSword`, the drop slot), nothing changes in play (one melee skill, the kind was the item type's already). Not changed: 7408 is on the item fusion list like 7404, and the base fusion takes three items by id. Tests: `unique_weapon_base_test.lua`, `unique_equip_test.lua`, `jewelry_test.lua`. Patch note 2026-11-13. server/README.md "Unique weapons and their base (O520)" |

| O437 | Owner 2026-11-09 (pt-BR): foco no lançamento e métricas; before the 2026-11-20 launch, D1 / D7 / D30 return rates for new characters, kept anonymous (no names, accounts, IPs or chat; extends O253 and the O258 / O374 funnel) | done in code and tests, not run live (Lua, Python, Rust; the Android card is not built here, no SDK) | Cohort by creation day (server day): the base `players` table has no creation date and no login history, so the returns are counted when they happen. A login k days after the first one counts each window once per character (bits of new storage 7102835): `d1` (the next calendar day, the real D1), `d2_7`, `d8_30`, and `in7` / `in30` (at least one login on days 1-7 / 1-30; their own bits, since d1 + d2_7 would count a character twice). Named for what they measure: `in7` is not called D7. | O498 | Owner 2026-11-11 (translated): "It is not possible to write in the chat. When we click to change the tab in it, it gets stuck in the drag function." | done in code and tests, not run in a client | Two defects in the floating chat of O414, both since its first client package (winpkg-107). (1) Stuck drag: the header buttons (Channels, Private, X, gear) are `UIButton`s inside the header. A press on one reached `header.onMousePress` (a button has no press handler) and began a window drag, but the release goes to the pressed widget first and `UIButton:onMouseRelease` returns `isPressed()`, true at that moment, so `UIManager::inputEvent` stopped there and `header.onMouseRelease` never ran. `drag` stayed set and every later move over the header moved the window. (2) No typing: the window was `focusable: false`; the console focuses its input with `recursiveFocus`, which skips an unfocusable widget, so the chain of focused children from the game root panel never reached the window and key events never reached the input (Enter did open the chat, the keys went nowhere). Fix: `FloatChatLogic.drag*` state machine (a press on a button never begins a drag; a drag ends on any release, on a move with the left button up (`g_mouse.isPressed`), when the client window loses focus (`g_window.hasFocus`), on hide and on logout; the poll drops a stale drag without a move); the window is made focusable once the console is in it, the header and the grip are not focusable, and the input takes the keys back after a click in the window or a menu choice. Same defect, same helper (`game_hudmove/dragguard.lua`, `ChaosDragGuard.onChild` / `.stale`): the DPS meter header, the party frames header and the Infinity Tower panel (buttons inside a panel that grabs the mouse: the mouse stayed grabbed). Checked and safe: chat panel grips, hotbar handle, goals title, HUD move (the drag source is the pressed widget, or `canStartDrag` keeps buttons out), map overlay (own `isPressed` check), walkpad (touch). Tests: `floatchat_test.lua`, `hotbar_session_test.lua` (real `floatchat.lua` on the widget mock), `game_hudmove/dragguard_test.lua`. See client/MANIFEST.md `game_floatchat`. |

| O501 | Owner 2026-11-11 (translated): "It seems the auto attack is hitting practically nothing on the monsters." | done in code and tests; measured on a local server 2026-11-11 (a live server has not run it) | Measured first (README "Monster defence (O462)", "Weapon hits (O501)"): bare characters of six kinds at levels 8 to 1000 against a dummy of their level, the layer on and off, a rare and a boss at 100 and 500, a real monster at 8 to 100. The O462 armour was sized for the median CLASS hit (a spell, after the player's multipliers); an auto attack arrives at the layer as the engine rolled it, 3 to 27 times smaller, and lost 37 to 81% (Warrior), 15 to 69% (Ranger), 45 to 90% (Monk) of its physical part on a normal monster, 65 to 90% on a rare, 72 to 90% on a boss; the Mystic's wand (`ORIGIN_WAND`) is never armoured. A second cause was there before O462: the monster's own XML armour and defence (the engine, before any Lua) blocked half of a level 20 Warrior's hits and took half of the rest. Fix: `weaponHitCurve` (the median ARRIVING weapon hit, derived by `tools/defence_curve.py` from the engine formula, the tier weapon, the physical share 65%, the skill floor 36 and the flat armour of a hunt monster), `weaponTarget` 27.5 / 44.5 / 55, `weaponShareCap` 40 / 55 / 65 in `monster_defence_config.lua`; `C.defend` picks the value by origin (a spell keeps the class curve: no spell number moves); `defence_curve.py --check` holds G1 (the median class loses 25 / 40 / 50% of its auto attack damage, within 2.2 points at 85 levels) and G2 (no class over 40 / 55 / 65%); `defence_curve_test.py` (CI). After the fix the same characters lose 6 to 40% and reach 60 to 90% of their auto damage (before 9 to 59%). Not done: the flat armour and defence of the monster files and the Monk's fist attack (engine, scaled after the flat armour): proposed in the README, for the owner. |

Both files are read in one streaming pass (24 MiB chunks, numpy); every tile outside the intended set is byte for byte the same (items, attributes, flags, house id); added, rewritten and removed tiles are as meant (with the exact bytes the writer built); no tile twice; header and the towns / waypoints nodes equal unless the writer frees them; both files parse to the end. | O560 | Owner 2026-11-14 (pt-BR): "quando a gente muda o auto build rápido, ele demora pra trocar e as vezes não troca" (the passive tree's Auto build dropdown). | done in code and tests, not run in a client | Cause: a preview request is sent per pick and the server dropped a quick second one without any answer (the generic 200 ms per-action limit of opcode 65 ran before the preview's own answered limit), then refused with "too fast" inside 500 ms; the client waited its 4 s timeout or a fixed 700 ms retry, with 3 tries, so a quick switch showed "Asking..." for seconds and sometimes ended in the error line with the old tree still shown. Fix: the pick shows at once; the client sends at most one request per 150 ms and asks for the LAST pick; every request carries `seq`, echoed in the answer, an answer to an older `seq` is dropped (`AutoBuildView.gateDelay`, `isStale`, `retryDelay`); `autobuildPreview` skips the generic limit, its own limit is 150 ms (`previewRateMs`) and a refusal says `wait`; another autobuild action refused by the generic limit is answered with `autobuildError`, never dropped. No new opcode. Tests: `tests/passive_builds_test.lua`, client `autobuildview_test.lua`, `autobuild_test.lua`. server/README.md "Auto build". |
